# Codex verification brief — relation map on the try-it page (commit bfcb83d)

You are an independent reviewer. Do NOT edit any tracked file. Write scratch files only under
`audit_evidence_claims/m1/`. Put your report in `audit_evidence_claims/m1/REPORT.md`.
End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.

## What was added

`src/app.py` `Engine.explore(text, per_group=30)` and `POST /api/explore`; `web/try.html` section "0. 관계 지도"
(`drawMap`, `explore`, `itemDetail`, the GKO/TKO/ACT/PH tables, click-through and the "따라온 길" trail);
`tests/test_app.py::test_explore_draws_a_drug_and_refuses_a_biosimilar`. Run tests with
`python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` (317). Start the page with `python src/app.py [port]` (use a port
other than 8765, which may already be in use) and exercise it over HTTP.

The page is for a domain reviewer to look at one node (drug, target, company, investigator, trial, disease) and
everything a live edge ties it to, grouped by relation, and to click through. It is read-only.

## The guarantees claimed (page text in section 0 and the `explore` docstring)

1. Every neighbour shown is joined to the centre by a live (not retired) edge to a live node, and the group label
   states the relation and its direction correctly (e.g. "이 약의 임상을 주관한 곳 (주관 ≠ 개발)" for
   Company -SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF-> Drug seen from the drug).
2. Details shown for a neighbour are the stored values: trial phases/status/start/title/url, sponsor trial count
   (`evidence_trials`) and sponsor class, drug trial count in the graph (EVALUATES), max phase, action type.
3. A name the question linker refuses (biosimilar, distinct product, tracer) is reported as refused, never drawn
   as the originator molecule; an unknown name says "not found".
4. Groups are ordered by size and each list keeps the `per_group` neighbours with most evidence; the page says
   when a list is cut and shows the true total.
5. Nothing is written; no model is called.

## What to verify (reproduce; cite file:line and exact inputs)

A. **Wrong relations or directions.** Any centre where an edge is grouped under the wrong label or direction,
   self-loops (src == dst), edges with both ends equal to the centre, PARENT_OF/SPONSORS/STUDIES/ASSOCIATED_WITH
   direction wording, Investigator vs Company handling, nodes whose type the tables do not cover.
B. **Name resolution.** `explore` takes the first non-refused hit of `link_entities`. Inputs with several entities
   ("osimertinib EGFR"), a refused token next to a real one ("Mvasi bevacizumab"), salts vs parents, company aliases
   with `also_matching_ids` (only the first id is drawn — is that misleading?), node ids vs names that collide,
   case/whitespace/Unicode, long inputs. Is anything drawn that the input does not name, or a refused product drawn
   as the originator?
C. **Wrong values.** Trial counts, sponsor counts, max phase and action shown versus the database
   (`data/processed/oncokg.sqlite`); retired edges/nodes leaking in; counts computed over retired edges;
   the `trials` count chunking; JSON props that are null or malformed.
D. **Cutting and ordering.** Is the "most evidence" ordering what the page says (trials are ordered by start date
   string — is that what the text implies)? Does the map's subset (`drawMap` allocation) ever hide a whole group
   or misstate totals? The Disease node (thousands of neighbours) — performance and output size.
E. **Page safety and correctness.** HTML/SVG injection from labels, titles, ids, URLs (`href` with `javascript:`?),
   edge ids in `data-id` attributes, the trail/back buttons after errors, double clicks, refused/not-found states,
   dark mode legibility is not in scope.
F. **Server.** Error handling for empty/oversized input, concurrency with the lock, behaviour when the DB has
   retired rows.
G. **Docs and wording.** Section 0 text, README/README.ko lines for `app.py`, the module docstring: do they claim
   more than the code does? Korean labels in GKO/ACT/PH that misstate the stored code.

## Approval bar

No known way for the map to show a relation that is not a live edge, a wrong direction or relation wording, a
value that differs from the stored one, a refused product drawn as the originator, or script injection. Report
should-fix items (misleading wording, cut lists without notice) separately from blockers.

| # | Finding | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| M17-B1 | "trastuzumab labelled-I-124": punctuation was removed before the letter-boundary check, gluing the I to "labelled" | each word is also checked as its punctuation-separated pieces and every neighbouring pair glued ("I"+"124") |

Stored-alias sweep: no change. Tests 345.

 succeeded in 664ms:
---
name: review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes ??Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** ??does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** ??does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you ??run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point ??a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here ??not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) ??fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3.
- The brief: "Report ??per file/hunk where relevant ??every place the diff violates a documented standard. Cite the standard (file + the rule). Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings ??the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes ??that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing ??**Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions ??**Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

 # Independent relation-map review — round 17

Reviewed **`4df54ae7f8bd16babc4ccb2dd0e8d4ce0314098b`**. **CHANGES REQUIRED: one new P1 originator-refusal bypass.** `trastuzumab labelled-I-124` passes every refusal stage and draws unlabelled TRASTUZUMAB with its clinical-trial evidence. The corresponding `labelled-124I` form refuses. The gap also exists in HEAD^: this is a newly reproduced inherited defect, not a demonstrated latest-patch regression.

All exact previously promoted M1–M16 name blockers are **FIXED**, including M16-B1 `I-124-labelled trastuzumab`. **M16-S1's repeated isotope computation is FIXED**: the helper now performs one whole-item scan rather than one scan per matched occurrence. The explicitly accepted over-refusals and ChEMBL bevacizumab-code limitation do not determine this verdict.

## M17-B1 — P1: punctuation before a one-letter isotope loses its boundary

**Exact input:** `trastuzumab labelled-I-124`.

`POST /api/explore` returns HTTP 200 and draws **TRASTUZUMAB / CHEMBL1201585**, with **13 live clinical trials**, without any refusal. `Which trials evaluate trastuzumab labelled-I-124?` and `Who sponsors trials of trastuzumab labelled-I-124?` each return **12 originator evidence rows**. The same occurs for `trastuzumab labelled-with-I-124`, `iodine-I-124 trastuzumab`, and `trastuzumab (iodine-I-124)`.

The paired inputs `trastuzumab labelled-124I`, `I-124-labelled trastuzumab`, and `trastuzumab labelled with I-124` all refuse, and both QA questions have zero evidence. Bare `trastuzumab` remains available. These are one-product descriptions: the explicit word "labelled" and the I-124 isotope identify radiolabelled trastuzumab. This is not the explicitly excluded cross-word one-letter case: the isotope is wholly inside the original token `labelled-I-124`, and its preceding boundary is a visible hyphen.

Fresh primary scientific identity evidence: the original study constructed iodine-124-labelled trastuzumab for PET imaging, so the radioligand is not interchangeable with unlabelled trastuzumab. [Guo et al., original study, PMID 31919745](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31919745/). The NCI also uses element-first I-124 notation for an iodine radioconjugate. [NCI Drug Dictionary](https://www.cancer.gov/publications/dictionaries/cancer-drug/def/iodine-i-124-labeled-hsp90-inhibitor-puh71). These establish the isotope/product distinction and notation; no medical recommendation is made.

| Stage | `trastuzumab labelled-I-124` | `trastuzumab labelled-124I` |
|---|---|---|
| Raw resolver | CONTAINED_ALIAS + UNRESOLVED; no BLOCKED component | CONTAINED_ALIAS + UNRESOLVED |
| `_qualified` | false | false |
| `_resolver_blocked` | `{}` | `{}` |
| Actual `_marked` call | `None`; both tokens in segment 0 | `trastuzumab` refusal |
| `_item_has_isotope` | false | true |
| `_whole_name` | `None` | `None` |
| Public linker | Drug TRASTUZUMAB, no Refused | Refused only |
| Map | CHEMBL1201585, 13 trials | refused |
| Trial/sponsor QA | 12 / 12 | 0 / 0 |

**Cause:** `src/query.py:164` first converts every original token to its letters-and-digits key. At `src/query.py:167`, the new `(?<![a-z])([a-z])(\d{1,3})(?!\d)` guard then sees `labelledi124`. The preceding hyphen no longer exists, so `i` is incorrectly treated as internal to a letter run. `_medical_isotope_in` still recognizes only two-letter element-first forms (`src/query.py:147-149`); no earlier helper catches this spelling. `_qualified` (`src/query.py:204-226`) and `_resolver_blocked` (`src/query.py:275-317`) also miss. The earlier `_marked` neighbour loops start at `labelled` and cannot recover the trailing element-first label (`src/query.py:358-385`). The item guard at `src/query.py:395-397` therefore returns false, and `Engine.explore` selects the surviving positive hit (`src/app.py:88-95`). [Helper evidence](attacks/helper_evidence.json).

**Introduction:** both current and HEAD^ link/map/QA results reproduce this leak. The latest change repairs the earlier spelling but leaves this inherited boundary gap. The original approval bar still forbids the originator map.

**Corroborations, not extra findings:** uppercase/lowercase, underscore, colon, attached parentheses/brackets, and reversed comma/and list orders have the same result. `vinblastine labelled-H3`, `erlotinib labelled-F18`, `paclitaxel labelled-C14`, and `cetuximab labelled-Y-90` also draw their unlabelled drugs; mass-first and bare element-first controls refuse. Their token shapes support the common code cause; only the primary iodine-124/trastuzumab scientific identity was independently sourced. No claim that each synthetic spelling is a real marketed product is needed.

**Required result:** retain genuine within-token punctuation boundaries when checking element-first one-letter isotope labels, while preserving BKM120/AZD9291 and dose/schedule controls. Check all public resolver/linker/map/QA paths with these paired inputs. No repair was implemented.

## Independent HTTP confirmation

Their mass-first or whitespace-separated isotope controls refuse. `vinblastine tritium-H-3` similarly draws CHEMBL159, while `vinblastine tritium-3H` refuses. Separate-list reversals show that the tracer remains an accepted Drug even when OSIMERTINIB is selected first. BKM120, AZD9291 and `osimertinib 80 mg` remain available. [Requests and complete responses](root_checks/independent_m17_http.json), [server lifecycle](root_checks/independent_m17_lifecycle.json), [source adaptation](root_checks/independent_m17_manifest.json).

The separate DB/UI reviewer independently traces the same lost boundary in `iodine-I-124-labelled trastuzumab` and `trastuzumab labelled with iodine-I-124`. Both current and HEAD^ produce 13 map trials and 12/12 QA evidence. This corroborates the primary finding; it is not counted as another blocker. [Adjudication](db_ui/adjudication.md), [stage captures](db_ui/adjudication_tracer.json).

### M16-S1: the item's isotope state is now computed once

**FIXED for the exact reported extra work.** At 125 / 1,000 occurrences, instrumentation records **one whole-item scan** and **125 / 1,000 within-word helper calls**, respectively. The prior reproduction recorded 125 / 1,000 whole-item scans and 15,625 / 1,000,000 within-word calls. `@functools.lru_cache(maxsize=4096)` caches the pure tuple-keyed computation (`src/query.py:154-170`), called from `_marked` at `src/query.py:395-397`. The same single ISATUXIMAB entity survives. [Fresh instrumentation](cost/repeated_match_instrumentation.json), [reused script](cost/repeated_match_followup.py).

For the legal 3,999-character **answer** field, the fresh HTTP check takes **2.38207 s**, queues EGFR for **2.29969 s**, and takes **2.65707 s** warm. The ordinary map after completion takes 0.04031 s; all return 200. This verifies the repair without claiming all repeated-match work was removed. `_marked` still constructs/scans token lists before checking the cache and runs before entity deduplication (`src/query.py:340-368,553-563`). [HTTP](cost/isolated_repeated_match_cost_http.json), [closed owned server / unchanged DB](cost/repeated_match_cost_lifecycle.json).

`engine.lock` serializes API operations (`src/app.py:247-263`). These 3,999/4,000-character workloads use direct linking or the **answer field**; explore correctly rejects more than 500 characters. Limits precede NFKC (`src/app.py:239-243,260`; `src/query.py:117-118,254-256`). Residual costs are should-fix observations, not the reason for the verdict. **No model calls or model cost were incurred.**

## Specification

Spec source: the user's Round 17 brief and original relation-map guarantees; baseline `bfcb83d` to HEAD `4df54ae7f8bd16babc4ccb2dd0e8d4ce0314098b`.

The round requires "element-first one-letter isotopes inside one word (I-124, F18, C14, Y-90) count". The original approval bar forbids a refused tracer being drawn as the originator.

**Wrong/partial behavior, P1:** `trastuzumab labelled-I-124` contains I-124 inside one original word, visibly separated from the preceding letters by a hyphen. Every refusal stage misses, and the actual HTTP map draws unlabelled TRASTUZUMAB / CHEMBL1201585 with 13 clinical trials. Trial and sponsor QA each return 12 originator evidence rows. `trastuzumab labelled-124I`, `I-124-labelled trastuzumab`, and `trastuzumab labelled with I-124` refuse. In `src/query.py:164-169`, punctuation is removed before the negative letter lookbehind, so the hyphen-delimited I becomes a letter inside the flattened run `labelledi124`. This is an inherited gap present in HEAD^, newly reproduced here, rather than a demonstrated newly introduced leak. New stage captures, exact HTTP requests, and paired counterfactuals are in [new_results.json](attacks/new_results.json) / [new_http.json](attacks/new_http.json).

**Repair success:** the 42 original m16 attack/control cases were rerun, and original M16-B1 forms now refuse. Original M15 chelator/lowercase cases remain refused. Ordinary BKM120/AZD9291, dose/schedule, and bare-drug controls survive. The 428 transformed aliases over 104 keys have zero changed centre/refusal outcomes from m16. Cached item-state performance is measured separately by the root.

**Scope:** no tracked edits or missing spec were observed. Recorded over-refusals and ChEMBL biosimilar-code curation are outside blocker scope. No independent additional blocker is inferred from the same-family corroborations.

Specification: **one newly promoted P1 family**, sufficient for CHANGES REQUIRED.

## Integrity and reproduction

`Engine.ask` passes `use_llm=False` (`src/app.py:181`). Mutation fixtures use in-memory copies. The application's SQLite connection is not globally read-only; no-write conclusions rely on the inspected/executed paths and before/after hashes. Final verification compares **6,119 tracked-file SHA-256 hashes**, unchanged HEAD, clean tracked/staged diffs, unchanged database bytes, report links, and closed owned ports. [Initial snapshot](integrity_before.json), [final verification](integrity_final.json). DB SHA-256: `7341c5a7089731f67356da5a588b269d4b69e8e6c4c054acf19aa4ef2837b529`.

Run from repository root with bytecode disabled and temporary paths inside each m17 family's `tmp/`. Shared-port suites must run sequentially; cost must run after other workloads. The manifests retain original/adapted hashes and path/port changes. The name worker caught an accidental scratch-only port substitution inside the `DX-8951F` control, restored the original input, and reran the affected probe; final dispositions use corrected outputs. [Literal-preservation audit](names/adaptation_literal_check.json).

FILE audit_evidence_claims/m16/prepare_root.py 
 """Snapshot tracked bytes and adapt previous root/cost reproducers, never application code."""
from pathlib import Path
import datetime, hashlib, json, subprocess

OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT = OUT.parents[1]

def git(*args):
    p = subprocess.run(['git', *args], cwd=ROOT, capture_output=True, check=True)
    return p.stdout.decode('utf8')

snapshot = OUT / 'integrity_before.json'
if not snapshot.exists():
    paths = git('ls-files', '-z').split('\0')
    hashes = {p: hashlib.sha256((ROOT/p).read_bytes()).hexdigest() for p in paths if p}
    snapshot.write_text(json.dumps(dict(time=datetime.datetime.now().astimezone().isoformat(),
        head=git('rev-parse','HEAD').strip(), status=git('status','--short'), tracked_sha256=hashes,
        db_sha256=hashlib.sha256((ROOT/'data/processed/oncokg.sqlite').read_bytes()).hexdigest()), indent=2), encoding='utf8')
manifest=[]
for family in ['root_checks', 'cost']:
    dest=OUT/family
    dest.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
    (dest/'tmp').mkdir(exist_ok=True)
    for source in (OUT.parent/'m15'/family).glob('*.py'):
        code=source.read_text(encoding='utf8')
        # Existing readers of earlier round baselines remain unchanged.
        # All writers in these scripts use __file__-relative OUT.
        target=dest/source.name
        if target.exists():
            raise RuntimeError(f'Refusing to overwrite {target}')
        target.write_text(code,encoding='utf8')
        manifest.append(dict(source=str(source.relative_to(ROOT)),copy=str(target.relative_to(ROOT)),
            sha256=hashlib.sha256(source.read_bytes()).hexdigest(),changes=[],
            output_root='__file__ relative m16; original historical case inputs retained'))
(OUT/'reuse_manifest_root.json').write_text(json.dumps(manifest,indent=2),encoding='utf8')
print(json.dumps({'tracked_files':len(hashes),'copies':len(manifest),'head':git('rev-parse','HEAD').strip()}))

 """Sequential test and real-HTTP reproduction suite."""
from pathlib import Path
import json,os,subprocess,sys,time
OUT=Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT=OUT.parents[2]
env=dict(os.environ,PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE='1',PYTHONUTF8='1',PYTHONIOENCODING='utf-8',TMP=str(OUT/'tmp'),TEMP=str(OUT/'tmp'),TMPDIR=str(OUT/'tmp'))
jobs=[['run_replays.py','tests'],['run_replays.py','http'],['run_remaining.py'],['new_leaks_http.py']]
rows=[]
for args in jobs:
 start=time.perf_counter()
 p=subprocess.run([sys.executable,'-B',str(OUT/args[0]),*args[1:]],cwd=ROOT,env=env,capture_output=True,timeout=900)
 (OUT/(args[0]+'.batch.txt')).write_bytes(p.stdout+p.stderr)
 row=dict(args=args,exit_code=p.returncode,seconds=time.perf_counter()-start);rows.append(row)
 (OUT/'execution_all.json').write_text(json.dumps(rows,indent=2),encoding='utf8')
 print(json.dumps(row),flush=True)
 if p.returncode:raise SystemExit(p.returncode)

for name in ['run_rounds12_13.py','independent_m14_http.py','independent_m15_http.py']:

FILE root_checks/run_verification.py 
 """Run the unmodified existing suite with scratch confined to this directory."""
import hashlib
import json
import os
from pathlib import Path
import subprocess
import sys
import time

OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT = OUT.parents[2]
TMP = OUT / "tmp"
TMP.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
env = {**os.environ, "PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE": "1", "PYTHONUTF8": "1",
       "PYTHONIOENCODING": "utf-8", "TMP": str(TMP), "TEMP": str(TMP),
       "TMPDIR": str(TMP)}

def git(*args):
    result = subprocess.run(["git", *args], cwd=ROOT, capture_output=True)
    return {"code": result.returncode, "stdout": result.stdout.decode("utf-8", "replace"),
            "stderr": result.stderr.decode("utf-8", "replace")}

files = ["src/app.py", "src/query.py", "web/try.html", "tests/test_app.py",
         "data/processed/oncokg.sqlite"]
before = {p: hashlib.sha256((ROOT / p).read_bytes()).hexdigest() for p in files}
(OUT / "verification_initial.json").write_text(json.dumps({"head": git("rev-parse", "HEAD"),
    "status": git("status", "--short"), "hashes": before}, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
cmd = [sys.executable, "-m", "pytest", "tests", "-q", "-p", "no:cacheprovider"]
start = time.perf_counter()
run = subprocess.run(cmd, cwd=ROOT, env=env, capture_output=True)
(OUT / "pytest.txt").write_bytes(run.stdout + run.stderr)
after = {p: hashlib.sha256((ROOT / p).read_bytes()).hexdigest() for p in files}
summary = {"command": "python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider",
           "environment": {k: env[k] for k in ("PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE", "PYTHONUTF8", "PYTHONIOENCODING", "TMP", "TEMP", "TMPDIR")},
           "exit_code": run.returncode, "wall_seconds": round(time.perf_counter() - start, 3),
           "hashes_unchanged": before == after, "hashes_after": after}
(OUT / "pytest_summary.json").write_text(json.dumps(summary, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
print((run.stdout + run.stderr).decode("utf-8", "replace"))
print(json.dumps(summary, indent=2))
raise SystemExit(run.returncode)


FILE cost/run_all.py 
 """Run copied historical workloads and Round11 controls sequentially."""
from pathlib import Path
import hashlib,json,os,subprocess,sys,time
OUT=Path(__file__).resolve().parent;ROOT=OUT.parents[2]
env=dict(os.environ,PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE='1',PYTHONUTF8='1',PYTHONIOENCODING='utf-8',TEMP=str(OUT/'tmp'),TMP=str(OUT/'tmp'),TMPDIR=str(OUT/'tmp'))
rows=[]
names=['run_perf_current.py','perf_check_http.py','cost_stress.py','fresh_cost.py','unique_piece_cost.py','unique_cost_http.py','normalization_cost.py']
for name in names:
 start=time.perf_counter()
 try:
  p=subprocess.run([sys.executable,'-B',str(OUT/name)],cwd=ROOT,env=env,capture_output=True,timeout=240)
  (OUT/(name+'.stdout.txt')).write_bytes(p.stdout+p.stderr)
  row={'script':name,'exit_code':p.returncode,'seconds':time.perf_counter()-start}
 except subprocess.TimeoutExpired as e:
  (OUT/(name+'.stdout.txt')).write_bytes((e.stdout or b'')+(e.stderr or b''))
  row={'script':name,'timeout_seconds':240,'seconds':time.perf_counter()-start}
 rows.append(row);(OUT/'execution.json').write_text(json.dumps(rows,indent=2),encoding='utf8');print(json.dumps(row),flush=True)
 if row.get('exit_code')!=0:raise SystemExit(1)

FILE cost/repeated_match_followup.py 
 """Bounded causal/cost follow-up for the changed repeated-match workload."""
from pathlib import Path
import json,os,subprocess,sys,time,types
OUT=Path(__file__).resolve().parent;ROOT=OUT.parents[2]
sys.path.insert(0,str(ROOT/'src'))
import query
rows=[]
for n in [125,1000]:
    kg=query.OncoKG();kg.conn.execute('PRAGMA query_only=ON')
    original=query._medical_isotope_in
    calls={'one_letter_false':0,'one_letter_true':0,'characters':0}
    def counted(key,one_letter=True):
        calls['one_letter_true' if one_letter else 'one_letter_false']+=1
        calls['characters']+=len(key)
        return original(key,one_letter)
    query._medical_isotope_in=counted
    try:
        value=' '.join(['isa']*n)
        start=time.perf_counter();entities=kg.link_entities(value);elapsed=time.perf_counter()-start
        rows.append(dict(matches=n,characters=len(value),helper_calls=calls,seconds_with_instrumentation=elapsed,entities=entities))
    finally:
        query._medical_isotope_in=original;kg.conn.close()
(OUT/'repeated_match_instrumentation.json').write_text(json.dumps(rows,indent=2),encoding='utf8')
source=OUT/'unique_cost_http.py'
code=source.read_text(encoding='utf8')
old="';'.join(f'isa x{i:04d}' for i in range(400))"
assert old in code
code=code.replace(old,"' '.join(['isa']*1000)")
code=code.replace('unique_cost','repeated_match_cost')
(OUT/'repeated_match_cost_http.py').write_text(code,encoding='utf8')
env=dict(os.environ,PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE='1',PYTHONUTF8='1',PYTHONIOENCODING='utf-8',
    TMP=str(OUT/'tmp'),TEMP=str(OUT/'tmp'),TMPDIR=str(OUT/'tmp'))
p=subprocess.run([sys.executable,'-B',str(OUT/'repeated_match_cost_http.py')],cwd=ROOT,env=env,capture_output=True,timeout=90)
(OUT/'repeated_match_cost_http.py.stdout.txt').write_bytes(p.stdout+p.stderr)
(OUT/'repeated_match_execution.json').write_text(json.dumps(dict(exit_code=p.returncode,
    source='audit_evidence_claims/m16/cost/unique_cost_http.py',
    changes=['exact answer replaced by 1000 space-separated isa tokens','unique_cost output prefix replaced by repeated_match_cost']),indent=2),encoding='utf8')
print(json.dumps(rows,indent=2));print(p.stdout.decode('utf8','replace'))
raise SystemExit(p.returncode)

+"""Snapshot tracked content and reuse earlier audit scripts in this scratch root."""
+from pathlib import Path
+import datetime, hashlib, json, subprocess
+
+OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
+ROOT = OUT.parents[1]
+def git(*args):
+    return subprocess.check_output(['git', *args], cwd=ROOT).decode('utf8')
+def digest(p):
+    return hashlib.sha256(p.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
+snapshot=OUT/'integrity_before.json'
+if not snapshot.exists():
+    hashes={p:digest(ROOT/p) for p in git('ls-files','-z').split('\0') if p}
+    snapshot.write_text(json.dumps(dict(time=datetime.datetime.now().astimezone().isoformat(),
+        head=git('rev-parse','HEAD').strip(), status=git('status','--short'),
+        tracked_sha256=hashes, db_sha256=digest(ROOT/'data/processed/oncokg.sqlite')),indent=2),encoding='utf8')
+manifest=[]
+for family in ['root_checks','cost']:
+    dest=OUT/family
+    dest.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
+    (dest/'tmp').mkdir(exist_ok=True)
+    for source in (OUT.parent/'m17'/family).glob('*.py'):
+        target=dest/source.name
+        if target.exists():
+            raise RuntimeError(f'Refusing overwrite: {target}')
+        target.write_bytes(source.read_bytes())
+        manifest.append(dict(source=source.relative_to(ROOT).as_posix(),copy=target.relative_to(ROOT).as_posix(),
+            source_sha256=digest(source),copy_sha256=digest(target),changes=[],
+            note='Output uses __file__; exact case literals unchanged. Historical baseline readers retained.'))
+(OUT/'reuse_manifest_root.json').write_text(json.dumps(manifest,indent=2),encoding='utf8')
+print(json.dumps(dict(copies=len(manifest),head=git('rev-parse','HEAD').strip())))

101: # every Unicode dash (category Pd) and minus sign, read as a hyphen
102: _DASHES = "[" + "".join(re.escape(chr(c)) for c in range(0x110000)
103:                         if unicodedata.category(chr(c)) == "Pd" or chr(c) in "\u2212\u207b\u208b") + "]"
104: 
105: 
106: def _plain(text: str) -> str:
107:     """Accents off, as the alias key does."""
108:     t = unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", text or "")
109:     return unicodedata.normalize("NFC", "".join(c for c in t if not unicodedata.combining(c)))
110: 
111: 
112: def _alnum_words(text: str) -> list[str]:
113:     """Letters-and-digits words, with every other character a separator."""
114:     return re.findall(r"[A-Za-z0-9]+", _plain(text))
115: 
116: 
117: def _fold(text: str) -> str:
118:     return re.sub(_DASHES, "-", unicodedata.normalize("NFKC", text or ""))
119: 
120: 
121: 
122: # Mass numbers of the isotopes used in medicine, per element (lower case).
123: # Radioactive labels only: stable 2H, 13C and 15N mark chemistry ("13-cis"), not tracers.
124: _MEDICAL_MASS = {"h": {3}, "c": {11, 14}, "n": {13}, "o": {15}, "f": {18},
125:                  "p": {32}, "s": {35}, "cu": {61, 62, 64, 67}, "ga": {67, 68}, "br": {76, 77},
126:                  "rb": {82}, "sr": {89, 90}, "y": {86, 90}, "zr": {89}, "tc": {99}, "in": {111},
127:                  "i": {123, 124, 125, 131}, "sm": {153}, "lu": {177}, "re": {186, 188},
128:                  "pb": {203, 212}, "at": {211}, "ra": {223}, "ac": {225}}
129: 
130: 
131: def _medical_isotope(label: str) -> bool:
132:     m = re.match(r"^(\d{1,3})m?([a-z]{1,2})$", (label or "").lower())
133:     return bool(m and int(m.group(1)) in _MEDICAL_MASS.get(m.group(2), set()))
134: 
135: 
136: def _medical_isotope_in(key: str, one_letter: bool = True) -> bool:
137:     """Is a medical isotope written anywhere in this run-together key, mass first ("89zr",
138:     "a33124i") or a two-letter element first ("lu177")? One-letter elements only when
139:     `one_letter` (within a word: "3hvinblastine"; across words "13 nsclc" is not N-13)."""
140:     for m in re.finditer(r"(\d+)m?([a-z]{1,2})", key):
141:         mass = int(m.group(1)[-3:])
142:         el = m.group(2)
143:         if mass in _MEDICAL_MASS.get(el, set()):
144:             return True
145:         if one_letter and mass in _MEDICAL_MASS.get(el[:1], set()):
146:             return True
147:     for m in re.finditer(r"([a-z]{2})(\d{2,3})(?!\d)", key):
148:         if int(m.group(2)) in _MEDICAL_MASS.get(m.group(1), set()):
149:             return True
150:     return False
151: 
152: 
153: 
154: @functools.lru_cache(maxsize=4096)
155: def _item_has_isotope(words: tuple) -> bool:
156:     """Is a medical isotope written anywhere in this list item? Worked out once per item.
157: 
158:     Two-letter elements are read across words ("trastuzumab Ga 68"); a one-letter element
159:     only inside one word, mass first ("3hvinblastine") or element first ("I-124-labelled"),
160:     so "13 NSCLC" is not N-13 and "BKM120" is not M-120."""
161:     if _medical_isotope_in(T.canonical_key(" ".join(words)), one_letter=False):
162:         return True
163:     for w in words:
164:         # the word as written, and its punctuation-separated pieces with each neighbouring
165:         # pair glued, so a boundary the punctuation marks is kept ("labelled-I-124")
166:         parts = [p.lower() for p in _alnum_words(w)]
167:         for key in [T.canonical_key(w)] + parts + [a + b for a, b in zip(parts, parts[1:])]:
168:             if _medical_isotope_in(key):
169:                 return True
170:             for m in re.finditer(r"(?<![a-z])([a-z])(\d{1,3})(?!\d)", key):
171:                 if int(m.group(2)) in _MEDICAL_MASS.get(m.group(1), set()):
172:                     return True
173:     return False
174: 
175: def _labelled(drug_name: str) -> bool:
176:     """Does a drug's preferred name carry an isotope (it is the radiolabelled product)?"""
177:     key = T.canonical_key(drug_name)
178:     if _medical_isotope_in(key):
179:         return True
180:     for m in re.finditer(r"\d+", key):         # element before the mass ("f18", "y90")
181:         mass = int(m.group()[:3])
182:         before = key[max(0, m.start() - 2):m.start()]
183:         if mass in _MEDICAL_MASS.get(before, set()) or mass in _MEDICAL_MASS.get(before[-1:], set()):
184:             return True
185:     return False
186: 
187: def _isotope_edge(word: str) -> bool:
188:     """An isotope label fused to the start or the end of one word ("89ZrDFO", "DFO89Zr").
189: 
190:     A one-letter element counts only where the case marks the boundary ("89FDFO"), so
191:     dose text such as "2cycles" or "5in" is not read as carbon or indium."""
192:     for i in range(2, min(6, len(word)) + 1):
193:         for lab, rest in ((word[:i], word[i:]), (word[-i:], word[:-i])):
194:             # the label must meet letters, not run on into more digits ("89Zr" is whole)
195:             if not rest or not T.is_isotope_label(lab):
196:                 continue
197:             if not (rest[:1] if lab == word[:i] else rest[-1:]).isalpha():
198:                 continue
199:             elem = re.sub(r"^[\d\s\-\[\]m]*", "", lab)
200:             if len(elem) == 2 and elem[0].isupper() and elem[1].islower():
201:                 return True                      # "Zr", "Ga", "Lu"
202:             if len(elem) == 1 and elem.isupper() and (rest[:1].isupper() if lab == word[:i]
203:                                                       else rest[-1:].isupper()):
204:                 return True                      # "89FDFO"-like, case-marked
205:     return False
206: 
207: def _qualified(words: list[str]) -> bool:
208:     """Does any part of this list item name a distinct product or a tracer?
209: 
210:     The unanchored patterns are tried on the whole item once; the anchored ones (a
211:     biosimilar suffix, a leading isotope) on every run of up to eight words, so the
212:     work grows linearly with the item."""
213:     def hit(text: str) -> bool:
214:         # the alias key ignores punctuation, underscores and accents, so the guards also
215:         # see the text with those folded ("bevacizumab-(awwb)", "bevacizumab_awwb",
216:         # "bevácizumab-awwb")
217:         plain = _plain(text)
218:         # and with every separator (punctuation, underscore, spaces) read as one space,
219:         # so "generic-version of", "albumin - bound" meet the multiword patterns
220:         return any(T.is_distinct_product(v) or T.is_radiotracer(v)
221:                    for v in {text, plain, re.sub(r"(?:[^\w\s]|_)+", "-", plain).strip("-"),
222:                              " ".join(_alnum_words(plain))})
223: 
224:     if hit(" ".join(words)):
225:         return True
226:     if any(T.canonical_key(w) in T.BIOSIMILAR_BRANDS | T.REFORMULATION_NAMES for w in words):
227:         return True
228:     return any(hit(" ".join(words[i:j])) for i in range(len(words))
229:                for j in range(i + 1, min(len(words), i + 8) + 1))
230:

232:     def __init__(self, db_path: Path = DB_PATH,
233:                  resolved_path: Path = C.PROCESSED / "resolved.json"):

257:     def link_entities(self, question: str) -> list[dict]:
258:         # one spelling for every check: compatibility forms folded, every dash a hyphen
259:         question = _fold(question)

278:     def _resolver_blocked(self, text: str) -> dict:
279:         """Drug ids inside the pieces the intervention resolver refuses as a distinct
280:         product or a tracer, with the refused piece."""
281:         comps = []
282:         lone = set()
283:         pieces = {p.strip() for p in re.split(r"[,;\uff0c\uff1b\u3001/|]", text) if p.strip()}
284:         for piece in pieces:                         # each distinct list piece once
285:             words = [w for w in re.split(r"[^\w\-]+", piece) if w]
286:             if not self._drug_spans(words, set(), [0] * len(words)):
287:                 continue                             # no drug in it: nothing to refuse
288:             got = self._resolve_cache.get(piece)
289:             if got is None:

292:                 except Exception:                    # never let a guard crash a lookup
293:                     got = []

295:                     self._resolve_cache.clear()

297:             if len(words) == 1 and T.canonical_key(piece) in self.dic["index"]:

308:                 continue
309:             if piece in lone:

313:             for a, b in self._drug_spans(words, set(), [0] * len(words)):

317:                     # anastrozole, Arimidex") is left to the list-item rule below

322:     def _keys_of(self, did: str) -> set:
323:         """Every lookup key that names this drug."""
324:         if not hasattr(self, "_drug_keys"):
325:             self._drug_keys: dict = {}
326:             for k, hits in self.dic["index"].items():

328:                     self._drug_keys.setdefault(h["drug_id"], set()).add(k)
329:         return self._drug_keys.get(did, set())

331:     def _marked(self, key: str, did: str, tokens, seg, a: int, b: int):
332:         """The text that makes this drug name a distinct product or a tracer, if any.
333: 
334:         Checked on the same letters-and-digits form the name was looked up by: a known
335:         biosimilar suffix at the end of the name ("bevacizumab awwb", "bevacizumab_awwb")
336:         or as the next word, or an isotope label just before or after it ("[89.Zr]
337:         trastuzumab", "89:Zr trastuzumab")."""
338:         for sfx in T.BIOSIMILAR_FDA_SUFFIXES:
339:             stem = key[:-len(sfx)]
340:             if key.endswith(sfx) and stem and any(
341:                     h["drug_id"] == did for h in self.dic["index"].get(stem, [])):
342:                 return " ".join(tokens[a:b])
343:         same = [k for k in range(len(tokens)) if seg[k] == seg[a]]
344:         before = [k for k in same if k < a]
345:         after = [k for k in same if k >= b]
346:         # words counted after normalisation, so punctuation never uses up the budget
347:         prev = _alnum_words(" ".join(tokens[k] for k in before))[-2:]
348:         nxt = _alnum_words(" ".join(tokens[k] for k in after))[:2]
349:         own = _alnum_words(" ".join(tokens[a:b]))
350:         lone = not before and not after      # the name is the whole list item
351:         # an isotope label anywhere in the name ("A33 124i"), or just before or after it
352:         # ("[89.Zr] trastuzumab", "trastuzumab 89 - Zr"); an isotope-shaped name counts
353:         # as a name only when it stands alone ("566C", "18f"), never as the substance
354:         # of a longer phrase ("18F fluorothymidine")
355:         inside = [own[i:j] for i in range(len(own)) for j in (i + 1, i + 2) if j <= len(own)]
356:         # and every run of the name's words glued as the key glues them, so a symbol
357:         # split by punctuation inside a stored alias is still an isotope ("[89Z.r]-DFO-")
358:         for i in range(len(own)):
359:             for j in range(i + 1, len(own) + 1):
360:                 glued = "".join(own[i:j])
361:                 if len(glued) > 6:
362:                     break
363:                 if j - i > 1:
364:                     inside.append([glued])
365:         if lone and T.is_isotope_label("".join(own)):
366:             inside = []                      # the whole item is one isotope-shaped name
367:         name = " ".join(tokens[a:b])
368:         # the neighbours glued back together the way the lookup key glues them, so a
369:         # suffix or an isotope spelt in pieces is still seen ("a - w w b", "[89Z.r]")
370:         nxt_all = _alnum_words(" ".join(tokens[k] for k in after))[:8]
371:         prev_all = _alnum_words(" ".join(tokens[k] for k in before))[-8:]
372:         for n in range(1, len(nxt_all) + 1):

375:                 return f"{name} ({' '.join(nxt_all[:n])})"
376:             if len(glued) > 6:

382:             if len(glued) > 6:

384:         # a label fused to a neighbouring word, such as a chelator ("DFO89Zr", "89ZrDFO")
385:         for w in nxt_all[:2] + prev_all[-2:]:

389:             if ws and T.is_isotope_label(" ".join(ws)):

391:         # an isotope glued to the name itself ("89zr-trastuzumab" read through "[89Z.r]")

FILE audit_evidence_claims/m18/root_checks/independent_m17_http.py 
 """Independent CLI HTTP confirmation of candidate round-nine findings."""
import hashlib
import json
import os
from pathlib import Path
import socket
import subprocess
import sys
import time
import urllib.error
import urllib.request

OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT = OUT.parents[2]
PORT = 8848
BASE = f"http://127.0.0.1:{PORT}"
CASES = ['trastuzumab', 'I-124-labelled trastuzumab', 'trastuzumab labelled-I-124', 'trastuzumab labelled-124I', 'iodine-I-124 trastuzumab', 'iodine I-124 trastuzumab', 'trastuzumab labelled-with-I124', 'trastuzumab labelled with I124', 'trastuzumab labelled-C14', 'trastuzumab labelled-14C', 'vinblastine tritium-H-3', 'vinblastine tritium-3H', 'osimertinib, trastuzumab labelled-I-124', 'trastuzumab labelled-I-124, osimertinib', 'BKM120', 'AZD9291', 'osimertinib 80 mg']


def post(endpoint, text):
    req = urllib.request.Request(BASE + "/api/" + endpoint,
        data=json.dumps({"text": text}).encode(),
        headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
    start = time.perf_counter()
    try:
        response = urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=30)
    except urllib.error.HTTPError as error:
        response = error
    with response:
        return {"endpoint": endpoint, "input": text, "status": response.status,
                "seconds": time.perf_counter() - start,
                "response": json.loads(response.read())}

with socket.socket() as sock:
    assert sock.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0, "owned port already occupied"
db = ROOT / "data/processed/oncokg.sqlite"
before = hashlib.sha256(db.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
env = {**os.environ, "PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE": "1", "PYTHONUTF8": "1", "PYTHONIOENCODING": "utf-8", "TMP":str(OUT/"tmp"), "TEMP":str(OUT/"tmp"), "TMPDIR":str(OUT/"tmp")}
with (OUT / "independent_m17_server.txt").open("w", encoding="utf-8") as log:
    proc = subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, "-B", "-u", "src/app.py", str(PORT)],
        cwd=ROOT, env=env, stdout=log, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
        creationflags=getattr(subprocess, "CREATE_NO_WINDOW", 0))
    results = []
    try:
        for _ in range(100):
            assert proc.poll() is None, "server exited"
            try:
                page = urllib.request.urlopen(BASE, timeout=1).read()
                break
            except OSError:
                time.sleep(.1)
        else:
            raise RuntimeError("server unavailable")
        for name in CASES:
            for endpoint in ("resolve", "explore"):
                results.append(post(endpoint, name))
            if not name.startswith("CHEMBL"):
                results.append(post("ask", f"Which trials evaluate {name}?"))
                results.append(post("ask", f"Who sponsors trials of {name}?"))
        (OUT / "independent_m17_http.json").write_text(json.dumps(results,
            ensure_ascii=False, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
        print(json.dumps([{"input": r["input"], "status": r["status"],
            "center": r["response"].get("center", {}).get("id"),
            "refused": r["response"].get("refused")}
            for r in results if r["endpoint"] == "explore"], indent=2))
    finally:
        proc.terminate()
        proc.wait(timeout=10)
        with socket.socket() as sock:
            closed = sock.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0
        (OUT / "independent_m17_lifecycle.json").write_text(json.dumps({
            "pid": proc.pid, "port": PORT, "port_closed": closed,
            "database_unchanged": hashlib.sha256(db.read_bytes()).hexdigest() == before,
            "requests": len(results), "all_200": all(r["status"] == 200 for r in results),
            "page_matches_worktree": page == (ROOT / "web/try.html").read_bytes()
        }, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")


FILE audit_evidence_claims/m18/root_checks/run_http.py 
 """Round 5 HTTP and QA regression checks; all output stays beside this file."""
import ast
import hashlib
import json
import os
from pathlib import Path
import re
import socket
import subprocess
import sys
import time
import types
import urllib.error
import urllib.request

OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT = OUT.parents[2]
PORT = 8840
BASE = f"http://127.0.0.1:{PORT}"
sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT / "src"))
os.environ["PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE"] = "1"
DB = ROOT / "data/processed/oncokg.sqlite"

def git(*args):
    p = subprocess.run(["git", *args], cwd=ROOT, capture_output=True)
    return {"code": p.returncode, "stdout": p.stdout.decode("utf-8", "replace"),
            "stderr": p.stderr.decode("utf-8", "replace")}

def post(path, payload):
    req = urllib.request.Request(BASE + path, data=json.dumps(payload).encode(),
                                 headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
    try:
        res = urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=30)
    except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:
        res = e
    return {"status": res.status, "body": json.loads(res.read())}

before = hashlib.sha256(DB.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
initial = {"head": git("rev-parse", "HEAD"), "status": git("status", "--short"),
           "db_sha256": before}
(OUT / "initial_integrity.json").write_text(json.dumps(initial, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
with socket.socket() as s:
    assert s.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0, "audit port already occupied"

log = (OUT / "server.txt").open("w", encoding="utf-8")
proc = subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, "-B", "-u", "src/app.py", str(PORT)], cwd=ROOT,
                        env=os.environ.copy(), stdout=log, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
                        creationflags=getattr(subprocess, "CREATE_NO_WINDOW", 0))
try:
    for _ in range(100):
        if proc.poll() is not None:
            raise RuntimeError("server exited")
        try:
            urllib.request.urlopen(BASE, timeout=1).read()
            break
        except OSError:
            time.sleep(.1)
    else:
        raise RuntimeError("server unavailable")
    # Re-run every original HTTP case without executing a writer in m1.
    original = (ROOT / "audit_evidence_claims/m1/http_audit.py").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    adapted = original.replace("8876", str(PORT)).replace("bfcb83d", "514929c")
    (OUT / "http_audit.py").write_text(adapted.replace("OUT.parents[1]", "OUT.parents[2]"), encoding="utf-8")
    run = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-B", str(OUT / "http_audit.py")], cwd=ROOT,
                         capture_output=True, env={**os.environ, "PYTHONIOENCODING": "utf-8"})
    (OUT / "http_stdout.txt").write_bytes(run.stdout + run.stderr)
    assert run.returncode == 0

    regression = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-B", str(OUT / "regression_http.py")],
        cwd=ROOT, capture_output=True, env={**os.environ, "PYTHONIOENCODING":"utf-8"})
    (OUT / "regression_http_stdout.txt").write_bytes(regression.stdout + regression.stderr)
    assert regression.returncode == 0

    # Load the previous linker/answer module in memory; use the identical database.
    old = types.ModuleType("m2b_old_query")
    old.__file__ = str(ROOT / "src/query.py")
    sys.modules[old.__name__] = old
    code = subprocess.run(["git", "show", "bfcb83d:src/query.py"], cwd=ROOT,
                          capture_output=True, check=True).stdout.decode("utf-8")
    exec(compile(code, old.__file__, "exec"), old.__dict__)
    baseline = old.OncoKG()
    baseline.conn.execute("PRAGMA query_only=ON")
    html = (ROOT / "web/try.html").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    questions = json.loads(re.search(r"const EX2 = (\[.*?\]);", html, re.S).group(1))
    # Verify CLI/dashboard demos are covered by the page list, without running
    # their entrypoints (which write tracked report/dashboard artifacts).
    sources = {}
    for filename, variable in [("src/query.py", "questions"),
                               ("src/make_dashboard_data.py", "demo_questions")]:
        parsed = ast.parse((ROOT / filename).read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
        assignments = [n for n in ast.walk(parsed) if isinstance(n, ast.Assign)
                       and any(isinstance(t, ast.Name) and t.id == variable for t in n.targets)]
        value = assignments[-1].value
        if isinstance(value, ast.BoolOp):
            value = value.values[-1]
        sources[filename] = ast.literal_eval(value)
        assert set(sources[filename]) <= set(questions)
    answers = []
    for question in questions:
        previous = baseline.answer(question, use_llm=False).to_dict()
        current = post("/api/ask", {"text": question})
        answers.append({"question": question, "http_status": current["status"],
                        "identical_to_bfcb83d": current["body"] == previous,
                        "current": current["body"], "previous": previous})
    baseline.conn.close()
    (OUT / "http_demo_results.json").write_text(json.dumps({"source_lists": sources,
        "answers": answers}, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False), encoding="utf-8")
    print(json.dumps({"demo_questions": len(answers),
        "all_http_200": all(a["http_status"] == 200 for a in answers),
        "all_identical_to_bfcb83d": all(a["identical_to_bfcb83d"] for a in answers)}))
finally:
    proc.terminate()
    proc.wait(timeout=10)
    log.close()
    with socket.socket() as s:
        port_closed = s.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0
    after = hashlib.sha256(DB.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
    (OUT / "http_lifecycle.json").write_text(json.dumps({"pid": proc.pid, "port": PORT,
        "server_exit": proc.returncode, "port_closed": port_closed,
        "database_unchanged": after == before, "db_sha256_before": before,
        "db_sha256_after": after}, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")

    report_final_line=report.rstrip().splitlines()[-1],report_sha256=hashlib.sha256((OUT/'REPORT.md').read_bytes()).hexdigest())
result['passed']=(not changed and not missing and not result['new_tracked_paths']
    and result['head_before']==result['head_after'] and result['tracked_diff']['code']==result['staged_diff']['code']==0
    and result['database_unchanged'] and all(ports.values()) and not broken and status['stdout']==before['status']
    and result['report_final_line'] in ('VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED','VERDICT: APPROVE'))
(OUT/'integrity_final.json').write_text(json.dumps(result,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2),encoding='utf8')
print(json.dumps({k:v for k,v in result.items() if k not in ('git_status','initial_status','tracked_diff','staged_diff')},ensure_ascii=False,indent=2))
assert result['passed'],'Final integrity check failed'

M17의 `trastuzumab labelled-I-124`는 현재 HTTP API에서 정상 거부됩니다. 다만 `trastuzumab radiolabelled with iodine-124`처럼 원소명을 풀어 쓰면 TRASTUZUMAB이 그대로 그려지는 것을 독립 재현했습니다. 같은 문장의 `I-124` 표기는 거부되므로, 각 검사 단계와 실제 방사성 표지 제품의 문헌 근거를 대조하고 있습니다.

"""Grounded question answering over the graph.

The retrieval step is the same resolver the pipeline uses, pointed at the
question instead of at a registry record. A question that names "Tagrisso" and a
graph that stores CHEMBL3353410 only meet if one vocabulary serves both, so the
dictionary is reused rather than reimplemented.

Three properties the answering layer holds to:

* **Every statement is traceable.** Each claim carries the edges it rests on,
  each edge carries its source record and fetch time.
* **The graph answers, not the model.** With a key, the language model is given
  the retrieved evidence and returns claims, not prose; each claim is checked
  against the graph and the answer is written from the accepted ones in the
  graph's own words (structured.py). With no key the evidence is rendered
  directly.
* **Nothing unsupported is shipped.** A claim the graph does not support is
  reported and left out. An unverifiable answer is worse than a short one.
"""

import os

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from pathlib import Path

sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))

+"""Snapshot tracked content and reuse earlier audit scripts in this scratch root."""
+from pathlib import Path
+import datetime, hashlib, json, subprocess
+
+OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
+ROOT = OUT.parents[1]
+def git(*args):
+    return subprocess.check_output(['git', *args], cwd=ROOT).decode('utf8')
+def digest(p):
+    return hashlib.sha256(p.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
+snapshot=OUT/'integrity_before.json'
+if not snapshot.exists():
+    hashes={p:digest(ROOT/p) for p in git('ls-files','-z').split('\0') if p}
+    snapshot.write_text(json.dumps(dict(time=datetime.datetime.now().astimezone().isoformat(),
+        head=git('rev-parse','HEAD').strip(), status=git('status','--short'),
+        tracked_sha256=hashes, db_sha256=digest(ROOT/'data/processed/oncokg.sqlite')),indent=2),encoding='utf8')
+manifest=[]
+for family in ['root_checks','cost']:
+    dest=OUT/family
+    dest.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
+    (dest/'tmp').mkdir(exist_ok=True)
+    for source in (OUT.parent/'m17'/family).glob('*.py'):
+        target=dest/source.name
+        if target.exists():
+            raise RuntimeError(f'Refusing overwrite: {target}')
+        target.write_bytes(source.read_bytes())
+        manifest.append(dict(source=source.relative_to(ROOT).as_posix(),copy=target.relative_to(ROOT).as_posix(),
+            source_sha256=digest(source),copy_sha256=digest(target),changes=[],
+            note='Output uses __file__; exact case literals unchanged. Historical baseline readers retained.'))
+(OUT/'reuse_manifest_root.json').write_text(json.dumps(manifest,indent=2),encoding='utf8')
+print(json.dumps(dict(copies=len(manifest),head=git('rev-parse','HEAD').strip())))

+"""Replay every late-round independent HTTP input against current HEAD."""
+from pathlib import Path
+import json, os, subprocess, sys, time
+OUT=Path(__file__).resolve().parent
+ROOT=OUT.parents[2]
+env=dict(os.environ,PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE='1',PYTHONUTF8='1',PYTHONIOENCODING='utf-8',
+         TMP=str(OUT/'tmp'),TEMP=str(OUT/'tmp'),TMPDIR=str(OUT/'tmp'))
+rows=[]
+for name in ['run_late.py','independent_m16_http.py','independent_m17_http.py']:
+    start=time.perf_counter()
+    p=subprocess.run([sys.executable,'-B',str(OUT/name)],cwd=ROOT,env=env,capture_output=True,timeout=300)
+    (OUT/(name+'.m18.stdout.txt')).write_bytes(p.stdout+p.stderr)
+    rows.append(dict(script=name,exit_code=p.returncode,seconds=time.perf_counter()-start))
+    (OUT/'execution_m18_late.json').write_text(json.dumps(rows,indent=2),encoding='utf8')
+    print(json.dumps(rows[-1]),flush=True)
+    if p.returncode: raise SystemExit(p.returncode)

286: # Isotope labels on radiotracers: 18F, 68Ga, 99mTc, 177Lu, 64Cu, 89Zr, 131I.
287: # These look exactly like development codes but name an isotope, not a molecule,
288: # and the substance they label is usually not in a therapeutic vocabulary at all.
289: RE_ISOTOPE = re.compile(r"^\[?(\d{1,3}m?)[\s\-]?([A-Za-z]{1,2})\]?$")
290: ISOTOPE_ELEMENTS = {
291:     "f", "c", "n", "o", "ga", "cu", "zr", "i", "lu", "tc", "in", "y", "at",
292:     "ac", "pb", "re", "sm", "sr", "ra", "rb", "br", "h", "p", "s",
293: }
294: 
295: 
296: def is_isotope_label(text: str) -> bool:
297:     m = RE_ISOTOPE.match((text or "").strip())
298:     return bool(m and m.group(2).lower() in ISOTOPE_ELEMENTS)
299: 
300: 
301: # "[18F]F-AraG", "68Ga-DOTATATE": an isotope prefix marks the whole string as a
302: # radiolabelled tracer, whose unlabelled moiety is not the administered drug.
303: RE_RADIOTRACER = re.compile(
304:     r"(?:^|[\s\(\[])\[?\d{1,3}m?[\s\-]?[A-Za-z]{1,2}\]?\s*[-–]\s*\w"
305:     r"|(?:^|[\s\(])\[\d{1,3}m?[\s\-]?[A-Za-z]{1,2}\]\s*[A-Za-z]", re.I)

308: def is_radiotracer(text: str) -> bool:
309:     t = (text or "").strip()

312:         return False

FILE audit_evidence_claims/m18/attacks/FINDINGS.md 
 # Round 18 attack and specification review

HEAD **4bed79c1f170ebe1efa3d452ce4398cce17b2113**; query-only counterfactual **4df54ae7f8bd16babc4ccb2dd0e8d4ce0314098b**. **One newly promoted P1 family: full element-name isotope notation passes every refusal stage and maps the unlabelled originator.** This is an inherited hole reproduced on both versions, not a demonstrated regression introduced by the latest patch. Original M17-B1 is FIXED.

## M18-B1 — P1: iodine-124 wording draws unlabelled trastuzumab

**Exact input:** `trastuzumab radiolabelled with iodine-124`.

The actual CLI server returns HTTP 200 for `/api/explore` and draws **TRASTUZUMAB / CHEMBL1201585**, with **13 live trials**, without a refusal. `Which trials evaluate trastuzumab radiolabelled with iodine-124?` and `Who sponsors trials of trastuzumab radiolabelled with iodine-124?` each return **12 originator evidence rows**. Changing only `iodine-124` to `I-124` or `124I` produces a refusal and zero trial/sponsor evidence. Bare trastuzumab remains available.

The input explicitly describes a radiolabelled product; it does not merely list iodine separately from trastuzumab. The original study constructed a PET radioligand by labelling trastuzumab with iodine-124, and PubMed indexes Iodine-124 as a substance for that study. This establishes the tracer/originator distinction for the primary example. [Guo et al., original study, PMID 31919745](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31919745/). The notation and clinical product distinction were freshly checked, not inferred solely from a spelling pattern. No medical recommendation is made.

The original approval bar says there must be no known way to draw a refused product as the originator. The paired symbol spelling of this same tracer is refused, whereas the ordinary full element name bypasses every stage. [Full stage records](new_results.json), [summary](new_summary.json), [actual HTTP requests/responses](new_http.json).

| Stage | `trastuzumab radiolabelled with iodine-124` | `trastuzumab radiolabelled with I-124` |
|---|---|---|
| Raw intervention resolver | CONTAINED_ALIAS + UNRESOLVED; no BLOCKED component | CONTAINED_ALIAS + UNRESOLVED |
| `_qualified` | false | false |
| `_resolver_blocked` | `{}` | `{}` |
| `_item_has_isotope` | false | true |
| Actual `_marked` call | `None`; four tokens, all segment 0 | returns refusal |
| `_whole_name` | `None` | `None` |
| Public linker | Drug TRASTUZUMAB; no Refused | Refused only |
| Map | CHEMBL1201585, 13 trials | refused, no centre |
| Trial / sponsor QA | 12 / 12 | 0 / 0 |

**Code cause:** the medical-mass table keys are element **symbols** (`src/query.py:124-128`). `_medical_isotope_in` only reads one/two-letter symbols (`src/query.py:136-150`); `_item_has_isotope` tries the canonical token, punctuation-separated pieces, and each adjacent concatenation, but never converts a full element name to its symbol (`src/query.py:154-173`). For `iodine-124`, the checked keys include `iodine124`, `iodine`, `124`, and `iodine124`; none becomes `i124`. The inverse regex may see `ne124`, and the one-letter inverse regex cannot treat the final `e` as a standalone element. [Exact helper inputs](helper_evidence.json).

Earlier layers also have no full element-name radiolabel rule: `_qualified` delegates to product/tracer patterns (`src/query.py:207-229`), whose isotope expressions accept only one/two-letter symbols (`src/textrules.py:289-314`). `_resolver_blocked` cannot block this input because its recorded components are CONTAINED_ALIAS and UNRESOLVED (`src/query.py:301-319`). `_marked` sees no recognised neighbouring/own isotope and the item check is false (`src/query.py:370-400`). `Engine.explore` therefore selects the surviving positive Drug (`src/app.py:88-95`). This is not a whole-name override, database alias corruption, or loss of a previously recorded refusal.

**Corroborations:** `iodine-124 trastuzumab`, `trastuzumab iodine-124`, `trastuzumab labelled-iodine-124`, `trastuzumab iodinated with iodine-124`, `trastuzumab (iodine-124)`, and `trastuzumab [iodine-124]` have the same wrong centre and 12/12 QA evidence. Uppercase, underscore and colon variants also bypass. Comma/and reversals keep the tracer as an accepted Drug even when OSIMERTINIB is selected first. Full-name zirconium-89/copper-64 forms behave the same, while their symbol controls refuse. Other synthetic element/drug pairs are common-cause probes, not claims that each is a marketed product or independent blockers.

**Required behaviour:** recognise this explicit iodine-124-labelled product consistently with its I-124/124I spellings; it must not draw unlabelled TRASTUZUMAB. Retain ordinary drugs, code/salt aliases and dose/schedule controls. No application repair was made.

## Earlier attack reproductions

All reused scripts retain their original case literals. Changes are limited to output names, module labels, owned ports, and the separate new input list. [Reuse manifest](reuse_manifest.json), [literal comparison](literal_preservation.json), [execution results](execution.json).

| Earlier reproduction | Status | Fresh evidence |
|---|---|---|
| M12 split-symbol stored alias and neighbour spellings | FIXED for promoted blocker forms | 41 focused cases in [focused_results.json](focused_results.json); accepted spelling supplements are not silently relabelled as fixed |
| M13 chelator/isotope prefix forms | FIXED for promoted blocker forms | 27 cases in [fresh_results.json](fresh_results.json) |
| M14 uppercase one-letter isotope / PET-CT suffix forms | FIXED for promoted blocker forms | 29 cases in [edge_results.json](edge_results.json) |
| M15 lowercase mass-first / isotope-chelator forms | FIXED for promoted blocker forms | 29 cases in [targeted_results.json](targeted_results.json) |
| M16 element-first one-letter isotope | FIXED | 42 cases in [previous_results.json](previous_results.json); [168 HTTP responses](previous_http.json) |
| M17 punctuation boundary before one-letter isotope | FIXED | All 63 original cases rerun. `trastuzumab labelled-I-124`, `iodine-I-124 trastuzumab`, and family now refuse; 24 prior leaked-centre cases improve. [Stages](m17_results.json), [252 HTTP responses](m17_http.json) |
| Transformed stored isotope aliases | No newly changed outcome | 428 variants / 104 keys; zero centre/refusal changes versus m17. [Comparison](alias_variants_comparison.json) |

The separate historical-name reviewer supplies the exhaustive m1–m17 per-finding dispositions, including accepted over-refusals and old nonblocking supplements. These grouped attack counts are not substituted for that table.

## Additional observations not separately promoted

- `trastuzumab labelled-I-1-24` still bypasses while `trastuzumab labelled-I-124` refuses: a reverse isotope spread across three punctuation pieces can escape the adjacent-pair budget. The split mass is an artificial spelling; it is not needed to establish M18-B1. The same applies to `labelled-C-1-4`. [Stage records](new_results.json).
- `trastuzumab labelled-I 124` bypasses. The documented item rule intentionally excludes one-letter symbols across whitespace to protect text such as `13 NSCLC`. This is recorded without calling that deliberate scope choice a new patch regression.
- A preferred-name `_labelled` inventory contains 14 visibly isotope-labelled drug names; no accidental exemption for a clearly unlabelled preferred name was observed. A synthetic `FDG labelled-C-14` selects the stored F18 product while `FDG 14C` refuses, because an already-labelled preferred name bypasses the item-wide guard. This is an unpromoted isotope-compatibility concern, not the originator blocker; product identity for that synthetic input was not independently established. [Inventory](labelled_preferred_names.json), [probe](labelled_exception_probe.json).

## Specification review

Spec source: the user's Round 18 brief and original map guarantees. The latest patch implements its stated punctuation-piece/adjacent-pair rule, and exact M17-B1 reproductions are fixed. No scope-creep finding is identified.

**One P1 specification failure:** the original approval bar forbids drawing a refused tracer as its originator. `trastuzumab radiolabelled with iodine-124` is the same labelled product whose `I-124`/`124I` spellings are refused, but all resolver/linker guards miss it, the live HTTP map draws CHEMBL1201585 with 13 trials, and trial/sponsor QA each supplies 12 originator rows. Full element names never become the symbol-keyed isotope labels in `src/query.py:124-173`; the positive hit survives at `src/app.py:88-95`. Fresh primary identity evidence and paired stages/HTTP are above.

Both current and HEAD^ query code exhibit the miss against the same current data/app modules. Thus the latest repair succeeds on its exact target while an inherited tracer-identity hole remains. Known over-refusals and ChEMBL bevacizumab-code curation do not determine this finding. **Specification: one newly promoted P1 family; CHANGES REQUIRED.**

## Execution and limits

Eleven probe/replay scripts exit zero: **231 historical staged cases + 64 fresh current/HEAD^ pairs**, **676 actual CLI HTTP requests**, all 200. Three owned ports (9130–9132) close; served page bytes equal the worktree; DB SHA-256 is unchanged in each server lifecycle. [Final assertions](final_checks.json), [new server lifecycle](new_lifecycle.json), [M17 lifecycle](m17_lifecycle.json), [M16 lifecycle](previous_lifecycle.json).

Direct paired probes use SQLite read-only connections. Counterfactuals change only the query module; they are not full old deployments. QA calls use the application's no-LLM path. No tracked file was edited; all attack artifacts are under m18/attacks. The scripts' timings overlapped other reviewers and are not isolated cost estimates.

## Limits and interpretation

Accepted known over-refusals and the ChEMBL bevacizumab-code data limitation remain nonblocking by the user instruction. Same-list-item refusal remains intentional. Earlier lower-case-ID/investigator lookup limits, non-parenthetical reversed-qualifier supplement, prefixed comma-name QA, fragmented qualifier supplements (`b.i.o.similar bevacizumab`, `lip.osomal doxorubicin`), and slash PET/CT not_found remain individually NOT FIXED. Their status is not silently promoted to repaired. The M17 control `cetuximab Y-90` still selects stored YTTRIUM Y-90 while disclosing refused cetuximab; it is not an every-stage-missed-refusal example.

Seven original QA demos retain **12, 12, 11, 1, 12, 0, 0** evidence rows. Six responses equal the bfcb83d-linker comparison; Mvasi differs in refusal metadata. [Demos](names_demo_results.json). Historical counterfactuals replace only their stated query method/module against current app/data; they are not whole historical deployments. Mutation fixtures use in-memory SQLite copies. No model is called.

The M17 repair is visible at `src/query.py:164-172`: each original word is checked in canonical form, as punctuation-separated pieces, and as adjacent concatenated pairs. The item result is used at `src/query.py:398-400`. Exact historical `trastuzumab labelled-I-124` now produces refusal, no map centre, and zero trial/sponsor evidence; ordinary BKM120/AZD9291 and dose/schedule controls survive. Cost is assessed by the root reviewer.

All new scratch files and temporary paths are under m18/names; bytecode is disabled. No tracked or application source was modified. Source report inventory is [recorded](historical_report_inventory.json).

+"""Assemble the audit from fresh reproductions and independent reviewer evidence."""
+from pathlib import Path
+import json, posixpath, re
+
+OUT=Path(__file__).resolve().parent
+def read(name): return (OUT/name).read_text(encoding='utf8')
+def data(name): return json.loads(read(name))
+def links(text, prefix):
+    def replace(m):
+        target=m.group(1)
+        if re.match(r'https?://|#',target): return m.group(0)
+        return ']('+posixpath.normpath(prefix+'/'+target)+')'
+    return re.sub(r'\]\(([^)]+)\)',replace,text)
+
+assert data('root_checks/pytest_summary.json')['exit_code']==0

+"""Snapshot tracked content and reuse earlier audit scripts in this scratch root."""
+from pathlib import Path
+import datetime, hashlib, json, subprocess
+
+OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
+ROOT = OUT.parents[1]
+def git(*args):
+    return subprocess.check_output(['git', *args], cwd=ROOT).decode('utf8')
+def digest(p):
+    return hashlib.sha256(p.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
+snapshot=OUT/'integrity_before.json'
+if not snapshot.exists():
+    hashes={p:digest(ROOT/p) for p in git('ls-files','-z').split('\0') if p}
+    snapshot.write_text(json.dumps(dict(time=datetime.datetime.now().astimezone().isoformat(),
+        head=git('rev-parse','HEAD').strip(), status=git('status','--short'),
+        tracked_sha256=hashes, db_sha256=digest(ROOT/'data/processed/oncokg.sqlite')),indent=2),encoding='utf8')
+manifest=[]
+for family in ['root_checks','cost']:
+    dest=OUT/family
+    dest.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
+    (dest/'tmp').mkdir(exist_ok=True)
+    for source in (OUT.parent/'m17'/family).glob('*.py'):
+        target=dest/source.name
+        if target.exists():
+            raise RuntimeError(f'Refusing overwrite: {target}')
+        target.write_bytes(source.read_bytes())
+        manifest.append(dict(source=source.relative_to(ROOT).as_posix(),copy=target.relative_to(ROOT).as_posix(),
+            source_sha256=digest(source),copy_sha256=digest(target),changes=[],
+            note='Output uses __file__; exact case literals unchanged. Historical baseline readers retained.'))
+(OUT/'reuse_manifest_root.json').write_text(json.dumps(manifest,indent=2),encoding='utf8')
+print(json.dumps(dict(copies=len(manifest),head=git('rev-parse','HEAD').strip())))

+         TMP=str(OUT/'tmp'),TEMP=str(OUT/'tmp'),TMPDIR=str(OUT/'tmp'))
+rows=[]
+for name in ['run_late.py','independent_m16_http.py','independent_m17_http.py']:
+    start=time.perf_counter()

+    (OUT/(name+'.m18.stdout.txt')).write_bytes(p.stdout+p.stderr)
+    rows.append(dict(script=name,exit_code=p.returncode,seconds=time.perf_counter()-start))
+    (OUT/'execution_m18_late.json').write_text(json.dumps(rows,indent=2),encoding='utf8')
+    print(json.dumps(rows[-1]),flush=True)
+    if p.returncode: raise SystemExit(p.returncode)
